## [LLMs Influence Human Spoken Language, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01754) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study provides empirical evidence that words preferentially generated by ChatGPT, such as 'delve' and 'meticulous', have significantly increased in spontaneous human speech since the chatbot's release. The analysis used a synthetic-control method on 737,083 hours of podcast conversations and a preregistered experiment with 496 participants. This finding demonstrates that large language models are not just passive tools but actively shape human language and culture, raising concerns about linguistic homogenization and the potential for a few AI providers to exert latent cultural influence at scale. The study analyzed 824,634 podcast episodes (737,083 hours) and used a synthetic control method to causally link the lexical shift to ChatGPT's release. A controlled experiment confirmed that a brief chatbot interaction led participants to adopt its words, persisting even after a distractor task.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Lexical shift refers to changes in word usage over time, often studied in sociolinguistics. The synthetic control method is a causal inference technique that constructs a counterfactual by weighting control units to estimate the effect of an intervention. This study applies these methods to measure the impact of AI on human language.

## [SNS-MDP Framework Proves RL Convergence in Non-Stationary Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18607) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the Switching Non-Stationary Markov Decision Process (SNS-MDP) framework, where the environment switches among a finite set of MDPs governed by a latent Markov chain. It proves that standard RL algorithms—TD learning, policy iteration, and Q-learning—converge almost surely under this non-stationarity. This work provides theoretical guarantees for RL in structured non-stationary environments, which are common in real-world applications like wireless networks. It bridges the gap between practical non-stationarity and theoretical convergence, potentially enabling safer deployment of RL in dynamic systems. The SNS-MDP framework assumes the agent observes only the external state, not the latent mode. The paper derives a closed-form expression for the value function under fixed policies and validates the framework on a wireless communication network with Markovian channel noise.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning typically assumes a stationary Markov decision process (MDP), but many real-world environments change over time. Non-stationary RL is challenging because standard convergence guarantees break down. The SNS-MDP model captures a common structure where the environment switches among a few modes according to a hidden Markov chain, allowing theoretical analysis.

## [GFWSVD: Scalable Kronecker-Factored Fisher for LLM Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17974) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Generalized Fisher-Weighted SVD (GFWSVD), a post-training LLM compression method that uses a scalable Kronecker-factored approximation of the Fisher information matrix to capture both diagonal and off-diagonal parameter correlations. At a 20% compression rate on MMLU, GFWSVD outperforms FWSVD by 5%, SVD-LLM by 3%, and ASVD by 6%. This work addresses a key limitation of existing Fisher-based compression methods that only use diagonal approximations, ignoring important parameter correlations. By enabling scalable off-diagonal Fisher information, GFWSVD can achieve better compression-quality trade-offs for large language models, which is critical for deploying LLMs efficiently. GFWSVD adapts the Kronecker-factored approximate curvature (K-FAC) algorithm to the observed Fisher information matrix for post-training compression. The method is demonstrated on LLM compression tasks and shows consistent improvements over diagonal Fisher and other SVD-based baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: The Fisher information matrix measures the sensitivity of model parameters to changes in the loss, and is used to guide compression by identifying less important weights. However, computing the full Fisher matrix is infeasible for large models, so most methods use a diagonal approximation that ignores correlations between parameters. Kronecker-factored approximations (like K-FAC) offer a tractable way to capture block-wise correlations by factorizing the Fisher matrix into Kronecker products of smaller matrices.

## [Fully Offline RL with Bayesian Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22442) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SOReL and TOReL, two Bayesian model-based methods for fully offline reinforcement learning that achieve minimax-optimal regret and enable complete offline hyperparameter selection without any online interaction. This work addresses a critical limitation of offline RL—the reliance on undocumented online interactions for hyperparameter tuning—making offline RL safer and more practical for real-world deployment in domains like healthcare, robotics, and autonomous driving. SOReL learns a posterior over dynamics and estimates policy value via predictive uncertainty, while TOReL extends the tuning framework to arbitrary offline RL algorithms. Both methods rely only on ensembling, introducing no additional computational overhead.

## [Verbalized Sampling Mitigates LLM Mode Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01171) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers identify typicality bias in preference data as a key driver of mode collapse in LLMs and propose Verbalized Sampling (VS), a training-free prompting strategy that asks the model to generate multiple responses with probabilities and then samples from that distribution. This work provides a new data-centric perspective on mode collapse and offers a practical inference-time remedy that significantly improves LLM diversity—by 1.6–2.1x in creative writing—without sacrificing factual accuracy or safety, which is crucial for applications requiring varied outputs. Verbalized Sampling prompts the model to output a probability distribution over a set of responses (e.g., 'Generate 5 jokes about coffee and their corresponding probabilities'), then samples from that distribution. Experiments show that more capable models benefit more from VS, and the method improves diversity across creative writing, dialogue simulation, open-ended QA, and synthetic data generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Mode collapse in LLMs refers to the loss of output diversity after alignment training, where models produce repetitive or generic responses. Typicality bias is a systematic distortion in preference data that favors common or typical responses over diverse or novel ones, rooted in cognitive psychology findings. Prior solutions often require additional training or complex decoding changes, whereas VS is a simple prompting technique.

Researchers propose Mixtures of SubExperts (MoSEs), a modular and sparse framework that enables lifelong learning in large language models by dynamically selecting and composing sub-expert modules to balance knowledge retention and acquisition. MoSEs addresses the stability-plasticity dilemma in continual learning, achieving state-of-the-art performance with bounded parameter growth, which is crucial for building foundation models that can continuously learn without catastrophic forgetting. MoSEs augments transformer layers with lightweight SubExperts and a learned sub-routing function that selects a sparse subset of modules per task, enabling sublinear growth in effective capacity and compositional generalization.

The paper introduces Diverging Flows, a method that enables flow-based conditional generation models to natively detect extrapolation (off-manifold inputs) by structurally enforcing inefficient transport for such inputs, without compromising predictive fidelity or inference latency. This addresses a critical safety issue in flow-based generative models, which otherwise produce plausible but incorrect outputs for out-of-distribution inputs, enabling reliable deployment in safety-critical domains like medicine, robotics, and climate science. Diverging Flows is evaluated on synthetic manifolds, cross-domain style transfer, and weather temperature forecasting, showing effective extrapolation detection without sacrificing performance. The method is model-agnostic and can be integrated into existing flow matching architectures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Flow Matching (FM) is a generative modeling paradigm that learns a continuous transformation from a simple distribution to a target distribution by regressing vector fields. It excels at conditional generation tasks like robotics and weather forecasting. However, FM models suffer from an extrapolation hazard: due to smoothness biases, they produce plausible outputs even for inputs far from the training data manifold, leading to silent failures. Diverging Flows tackles this by making the flow inefficient for off-manifold inputs, signaling extrapolation.

<a id="item-134"></a>
## [Looped Transformer Preference Encoding Corrected](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09870) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A post-publication audit of the paper "Relational Preference Encoding in Looped Transformer Internal States" found that the headline accuracies (95.2% pairwise evaluator, 84.5% pairwise probe, 21.75% pointwise probe) were inflated by two independent evaluation errors. Corrected results show modest but above-chance decoding: antisymmetrized evaluator accuracy is 63.9%, and pair-disjoint probe accuracies are 56.5% and 54.2%. This correction is significant because it demonstrates the importance of rigorous evaluation in interpretability research, especially for high-impact topics like preference encoding in transformers. The central finding that preference is encoded more accurately relationally than pointwise still holds, albeit with much smaller effect sizes, and the methodological contributions (e.g., flip test, swap-protocol metric deflation) remain valid. The 95.2% pairwise evaluator accuracy was a canonical-ordering artifact: the evaluator learned to prefer the first-presented argument. The 84.5% pairwise probe and 21.75% pointwise probe suffered from source-item leaks (orientation rows and pair partners crossing the train/test split). Corrected pair-disjoint values are 56.5% and 54.2%, both above chance, so the "inverted polarity" finding is withdrawn.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Looped transformers are a parameter-efficient architecture that repeatedly applies a fixed transformer block to mimic deep networks. The Ouro-2.6B model is a 2.6B-parameter looped transformer with iterative refinement. The Anthropic HH-RLHF dataset contains 170k human preference comparisons between two AI assistant responses, used for RLHF alignment. The paper trained lightweight evaluator heads on frozen loop-iteration states to decode human preference.

## [Generative Synthetic Data Can Distort Causal Estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23904) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that fully generative synthetic tabular data methods, including GAN- and LLM-based models, can preserve predictive utility while distorting average treatment effect (ATE) estimates. The authors propose a hybrid framework that generates covariates separately from treatment and outcome mechanisms to improve causal fidelity. This finding is critical because synthetic data is increasingly used for causal inference in sensitive domains like healthcare and economics, where valid treatment effect estimates are essential. The proposed hybrid framework offers a practical remedy that could improve the reliability of synthetic data for downstream causal analyses. The failure is structural: ATE preservation requires both a realistic covariate law and an accurate treatment-effect contrast, whereas prediction loss penalizes treatment-effect error only through an overlap-weighted term. The hybrid framework generates covariates while modeling treatment and outcome mechanisms separately, and is evaluated across settings varying overlap, covariate dimension, and sample size.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Average treatment effect (ATE) measures the difference in mean outcomes between treatment and control groups. Overlap weighting is a propensity score method that balances covariates by focusing on units with balanced treatment probabilities. Generative synthetic data models like GANs and LLMs are often evaluated on predictive performance, but this paper shows that such evaluation does not guarantee causal validity.

Researchers introduced Pipette, an embodied simulation platform, benchmark, and data augmentation framework for wet-lab robotics, providing over 100 open-source assets and a simulation-based pipeline to expand limited demonstrations into training data. Pipette addresses the critical need for scalable robot learning in biomedical experiments, enabling data-efficient training of vision-language-action models and lowering the barrier for non-experts to define new tasks. The platform supports three robotic-arm embodiments, integrates Tencent Hunyuan for 3D asset generation, and includes a 12-task benchmark. With only 30 demonstrations per task, simulation augmentation improved SmolVLA from 40.4% to 71.8% and pi0 from 37.3% to 44.1%.

**Background**: Wet-lab robots aim to automate biomedical experiments like pipetting and sample handling, but training them requires large amounts of diverse data. Simulation platforms offer safe, reproducible environments for data collection, but existing simulators lack editable laboratory assets and efficient augmentation pipelines. Pipette fills this gap by providing open-source assets and a simulation-based augmentation method that perturbs lighting, camera, speed, and actions.

TerraTransfer introduces a method to train end-to-end driving policies via self-play in vectorized simulators, then align with a vision backbone, eliminating the need for expert demonstrations. This decouples learning to drive from learning to see, significantly reducing training costs and enabling scalable RL-based autonomous driving without expensive data collection. The alignment uses action KL divergence and a batch-relational low-rank structural loss, requiring only paired (image, scene-state) data rather than expert trajectories.

## [VOiLA: Diffusion Models for Online POMDP Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19729) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VOiLA learns transition and observation models using conditional diffusion models, distills them into fast feedforward generators, and integrates with a GPU-parallelized planner (VOPP) for efficient online POMDP planning. This approach addresses the key bottleneck of obtaining accurate POMDP models for real-world robotics, enabling sample-efficient learning and strong generalization to unseen environments. The distillation reduces sampling cost by nearly three orders of magnitude, and VOiLA achieves equal or better performance than Recurrent Soft Actor Critic using less than 10% training data.

**Background**: A Partially Observable Markov Decision Process (POMDP) models decision-making under uncertainty where the agent cannot directly observe the full state. ## [Warning Labels Shift Perceptions but Not Influence of Sycophantic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21317) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A preregistered experiment with 2,610 participants found that warning labels describing AI as sycophantic reduce perceived objectivity and trust, but do not reliably reduce the AI's influence on users' self-perceived rightness or willingness to repair interpersonal conflicts. This reveals a critical gap between perception and influence, suggesting that warning-based interventions may offer a false sense of protection against AI sycophancy, which has implications for AI regulation and user interface design. The study tested three conditions: no label, basic AI disclosure, and a sycophancy-specific warning label. Only the sycophancy label shifted perceptions, but it failed to reduce sycophancy's influence on self-perceived rightness or conflict repair willingness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: AI sycophancy refers to the tendency of large language models to tailor responses to what users want to hear, even when inaccurate. Previous research has shown sycophancy can decrease prosocial intentions and promote harmful behaviors. Warning labels have been proposed as a mitigation strategy, but their effectiveness in reducing actual influence was unknown.

FlowWM introduces a stochastic world model that performs flow matching directly in pretrained DINOv3 feature space, enabling high-dimensional feature prediction without VAE compression or deterministic blur. This approach addresses the trade-off between perception quality and stochastic forecasting in world models, potentially improving downstream tasks like autonomous driving and robotics planning. FlowWM uses a differentiable one-step projection mechanism for efficient training with temporal consistency and task-driven objectives, and is evaluated on synthetic and real-world FuturePerception benchmarks.

**Background**: World models aim to predict future states of an environment from observations, but existing methods often use VAE-based stochastic models that lose perceptual detail or deterministic models that produce blurry averages. Flow matching is a generative framework that learns a continuous transformation from a simple distribution to a target distribution via an ODE. DINOv3 is a self-supervised vision model that produces high-quality, dense feature representations.

ShopX is a foundation model that unifies intent understanding, execution planning, and semantic ID-based item operations into a single model, enabling direct item-space fulfillment in agentic shopping workflows. This addresses a key bottleneck in LLM-based e-commerce by replacing lossy hand-offs between language understanding and item-space execution with a model-native approach, potentially enabling more natural and effective AI shopping agents. ## [Adversarial Pragmatics Benchmark for AI Safety Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01153) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces adversarial pragmatics as a benchmark and annotation protocol to systematically evaluate language model behavior under instruction conflict, embedded commands, and policy ambiguity. Current safety evaluations often compress nuanced failures into pass/fail labels, obscuring root causes; this benchmark provides a linguistically controlled taxonomy to distinguish capability limits, policy ambiguity, and instruction conflict, improving the reliability of AI safety assessments. The benchmark includes an 18-item seed set with validator-enforced metadata, a 54-row local seed pilot, and an expert-evaluation protocol that measures task success, policy compliance, safety risk, refusal outcome, and evaluator confidence.

**Background**: Adversarial pragmatics studies how linguistic phenomena like indirect speech acts, scope ambiguity, and deixis can be exploited to create conflicting or hidden instructions. ## [Study Questions Reliability of Coding Agent Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01211) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper audits three repository-level performance-optimization benchmarks (GSO, SWE-Perf, SWE-fficiency) and finds that most reference patches fail validity checks when replayed on different machines, with only 39/102, 11/140, and 411/498 tasks passing cross-machine validation respectively. These benchmarks are increasingly used to measure progress in AI coding agents, but the study reveals that leaderboard scores conflate runtime instability, scoring rules, and task saturation, undermining their reliability for evaluating agent performance. The study replayed 740 tasks across four Google Cloud machine types, finding that SWE-Perf is especially fragile due to near-zero runtime changes in many reference patches. Additionally, public submission rankings disagreed on 9 of 28 pairwise comparisons, and SWE-fficiency's scoring rule gave the worst ten tasks overly high weights (58.5%-82.8%).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Repository-level performance-optimization benchmarks evaluate coding agents by applying patches to real codebases and measuring runtime improvements. They are used to rank AI agents on leaderboards, but the study shows that results are highly sensitive to machine environment and scoring methodology, questioning their validity as reliable progress indicators.

Researchers propose CtrlBench-Rec, a collaborative multi-agent framework that systematically evaluates the controllability of black-box recommender systems through three formalized tasks: target content discovery, interest profile shaping, and popularity bias mitigation. This work addresses a critical gap by establishing controllability as a core evaluation dimension for recommender systems, enabling users and regulators to steer outputs and audit behavior, which is essential for transparency and fairness in AI-driven recommendations. ## [Agentic Coding Tools Adoption in GitHub Projects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14037) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A study of 25,264 agentic pull requests from 2,361 GitHub repositories reveals that agentic coding tools are used, but intensive adoption is concentrated in a small subset of projects, with small projects showing higher relative activity. This provides early empirical evidence on how open-source projects organize human oversight around agentic coding tools, highlighting that successful integration depends not only on agent capabilities but also on human and organizational processes. The median repository generates only one to two agentic PRs over three months, and human-agent collaboration is dominated by a single-human oversight model, with multi-human collaboration remaining uncommon.

The model, harness, and datasets are available under Apache 2.0. This release dramatically reduces the cost of agentic search inference—from requiring 16 GPUs to just one—while outperforming much larger open models like Qwen3.5-397B and Kimi-K2.6 on Recall@10. It makes advanced multi-step search economically viable for a wider range of applications. T-Search operates in rounds, transferring only a compact 'memory' between rounds to keep context within ~32K tokens. It supports flexible budgets: fast cheap search or three parallel rollouts with RRF fusion, boosting average Recall@10 from 55.96 to 61.33.

Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF) combines multiple ranked result lists into a single ranking by scoring documents based on their positions.

## [Three Non-Solution Responses to Problems](https://improvesomething.today/responses-to-problems/) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The article identifies three common responses to problems that avoid solving them: preserving the problem, ignoring it, or misdiagnosing it. It argues these patterns are prevalent in organizations and governance. This framework helps explain why many systemic problems persist despite resources being allocated to address them. It offers a lens for understanding organizational dysfunction and improving problem-solving approaches. The three responses are: (1) Preserving the problem, where stakeholders benefit from the problem's existence; (2) Ignoring the problem, often due to denial or lack of awareness; (3) Misdiagnosing the problem, leading to ineffective solutions. The article uses examples from government and business.

## [Brain Can Simultaneously Encode Two Speech Streams](https://journals.plos.org/plosbiology/article?id=10.1371/journal.pbio.3003876) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new PLOS Biology study using EEG recordings shows that the human brain can simultaneously encode two competing speech streams, even when attention is focused on one. This challenges the classic view that attention strictly limits speech processing to one stream at a time, with implications for understanding multi-talker environments and designing better hearing aids or brain-computer interfaces. Participants were cued to switch attention between two speech streams every 15–30 seconds in an immersive multi-talker environment, and EEG measured neural encoding of both streams simultaneously.

A new paper proposes a three-level hierarchical learning architecture for autonomous UAV swarms in search and rescue, integrating Hebbian plasticity, multi-agent RL with GNNs, and meta-learning with BDI reasoning, formalized through architectural contracts with safety guarantees. This architecture addresses fundamental limitations of existing hierarchical RL approaches by combining multiple learning paradigms with formal guarantees, potentially enabling more reliable and adaptive autonomous swarms for critical applications like search and rescue. The architecture includes 22 architectural contracts across six components, providing six classes of formal guarantees such as safety, liveness, and inter-level consistency. It also introduces Swarm Meta Cognition as a compositional property for monitoring and switching cognitive strategies.

Architectural contracts are formal specifications that guarantee system properties.

## [HG-RAG: Hierarchy-Guided Retrieval for Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14095) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HG-RAG is a new framework that traverses hierarchical knowledge graphs to retrieve structured context for LLMs, outperforming flat dense retrieval on hierarchical, relational, and multi-hop reasoning tasks. This approach addresses a key limitation of standard RAG systems that struggle with queries requiring hierarchical or relational reasoning, potentially improving accuracy and reducing hallucinations in complex question answering. The retrieval pipeline first resolves a named entity anchor from the query, then expands context upward (parent nodes), laterally (relational neighbors), and downward (child nodes) as needed. Evaluation was conducted across three world scales (18–800 nodes) with four query types.

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) combines LLMs with external knowledge retrieval to improve factual accuracy. Standard RAG retrieves from flat document stores, which fails to capture hierarchical or relational structures. Hierarchical knowledge graphs organize entities in parent-child relationships, enabling more structured reasoning.

A new methodology uses a panel of LLM-based AI agents to construct Bayesian Belief Networks (BBNs) for operational decision support, demonstrated on a healthcare case study. This bridges the gap between purely expert-driven and purely data-driven BBN construction, enabling decision support in domains with limited data. ## [ToolAnchor: Counterfactual Contexts Boost Agent Tool Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14145) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose ToolAnchor, a framework that injects counterfactual anchor contexts at critical decision points to help LLM agents overcome behavioral inertia and adapt to new tools without retraining. This addresses the toolset expansion problem, a key bottleneck for deploying LLM agents in dynamic environments where new tools are frequently added, enabling scalable agentic reinforcement learning without costly retraining. ToolAnchor uses a teacher model to hypothesize counterfactual contexts, verifies them via student rollouts, and internalizes successful interventions through agentic post-training. It was evaluated on GAIA, BrowseComp, and VDR-Bench tasks, showing competitive performance under expanded toolsets.

## [CatalogAgent: Supervisor-Mediated Self-Learning for GenAI Context Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14396) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CatalogAgent introduces a supervisor-mediated self-learning system that uses an LLM-based Generator and Evaluator, along with a Supervisor Agent, to resolve conflicts and improve structured attribute extraction for e-commerce catalogs. This system addresses a critical bottleneck in e-commerce catalog enrichment by enabling continuous self-improvement without human intervention, potentially reducing manual effort and improving product data quality at scale. The Supervisor Agent mediates conflicts arising from internal disagreements between Generator and Evaluator or external seller feedback, and its decisions are stored in a Memory Base and summarized into learnings that are fed back to the worker LLMs via context engineering, improving Generator and Evaluator performance by 15.24% and 13.98% respectively.

**Background**: Product catalogs often have missing structured attributes like material or color. LLM-based generator-evaluator frameworks can predict these attributes but face challenges when the two components disagree. CatalogAgent introduces a Supervisor Agent to mediate such conflicts and a memory system to enable self-learning, representing a new paradigm in context engineering for GenAI models.

## [Instrument Effects Skew Language-Model Honesty Evaluations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14399) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study demonstrates that changing the verdict grammar in a text-adventure honesty evaluation substantially alters measured language-model honesty, with strong claims dropping from 38/40 to 7/40 when a third verdict option was added. This finding highlights that evaluation instrument design can drastically affect AI safety assessments, urging the community to adopt rigorous protocols to ensure evaluations measure model properties rather than artifacts of the instrument. The study used a text-adventure world where the game engine knows quest outcomes, and found that a single sentence disclosing the success criterion reduced false verdicts from 18/59 to 0/58; repeated runs of a fixed configuration produced non-stable verdict distributions on 3 of 4 instances.

**Background**: Language-model honesty evaluations typically interpret a model's verdicts as evidence about the model itself. However, this paper tests the evaluation instrument instead, showing that design choices like verdict grammar can introduce systematic biases. The authors propose a four-check integrity protocol for evaluation instruments.

## [SAGA: Schema-Aware Grounding for Better SPARQL Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14494) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes SAGA, a training-free framework that improves type compatibility in agentic text-to-SPARQL generation by constraining property exploration with schema information. It achieves state-of-the-art results across nine benchmark settings on Wikidata and Freebase. SAGA addresses a critical limitation in KBQA agents—type-blind grounding—which often produces semantically invalid queries. By reducing empty-result queries and improving accuracy, it advances the reliability of knowledge base question answering systems. SAGA maintains a persistent bidirectional type state, filters incompatible property candidates at construction time, and presents graph patterns in a compact schema-annotated format. It handles missing schema information permissively using empirical and trace-local evidence.

**Background**: Knowledge base question answering (KBQA) aims to answer natural language questions using structured knowledge bases like Wikidata. One approach is semantic parsing, where questions are converted into executable SPARQL queries. It addresses a critical gap in managing multiple autonomous subsystems sharing physical resources. The IAL is domain-agnostic and represents intentions as first-class runtime objects. It uses a large language model grounded in an OWL ontology to parse natural-language goals into structured intentions, and a consistency monitor detects conflicts at registration time.

The IAL aims to bridge the gap between human intentions and low-level control.

## [Analytic Abduction: Causal Decomposition for Human-AI Coordination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14641) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a non-greedy, risk-sensitive approach to analytic abduction using a κ-τ apparatus for causal decomposition and governed commitment in human-AI coordination. It provides a formal framework that enhances the legibility of suspended reasoning, enabling human-AI teams to handle ambiguous situations without premature convergence, which is critical for high-stakes domains like epidemiology and cybersecurity. The framework's core is the κ-τ apparatus, where κ encodes epistemic interactions among hypotheses and τ sets a commitment threshold based on decision stakes, with a two-level architecture (intra-cluster κ* and inter-cluster κ**) to prevent causal misattribution.

**Background**: Abductive reasoning is a form of logical inference that seeks the most likely explanation for observations. Traditional synthetic abduction builds explanations from available hypotheses, while analytic abduction identifies latent factors behind complex observations. This work extends analytic abduction to human-AI coordination by introducing a structured commitment process.

Baseline results show benefits of multimodal fusion but reveal challenges under noise and low-light conditions.

## [RAPTOR: Reachability-Aware Pretraining for TKG Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14886) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose RAPTOR, a self-supervised pretraining method that injects a reachability-aware inductive bias into reinforcement learning agents for temporal knowledge graph reasoning, improving exploration efficiency and training speed. This approach addresses the critical issues of sparse rewards and inefficient exploration in RL-based TKG reasoning, leading to faster training and better performance on benchmark datasets, which could advance event forecasting and interpretable AI systems. RAPTOR learns to estimate the reachability of candidate actions to the target entity, reducing exploration of unpromising paths. It is evaluated on ICEWS14, ICEWS05-15, and ICEWS18 datasets, consistently outperforming conventional baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Temporal Knowledge Graphs (TKGs) store facts as quadruples (subject, relation, object, timestamp) and are used for event forecasting. Reinforcement learning-based multi-hop reasoning methods provide interpretable predictions by tracing paths, but suffer from sparse rewards and large action spaces. RAPTOR uses self-supervised pretraining to learn a reachability prior, which serves as a strong initialization for downstream RL fine-tuning.

## [LLM-Based System for Early Detection of Online Firestorms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14957) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a sequential LLM-based system for early detection of online firestorms, using three indicators: negativity share, escalation level, and contributor count. The system operates in two modes: a global mode for retrospective classification and an early warning mode for real-time detection. Online firestorms can cause significant reputational and economic damage, and existing detectors often miss contextual meaning shifts. This LLM-based approach enables context-aware monitoring and early warnings, potentially helping organizations mitigate harm more effectively. On a balanced Reddit dataset, the global mode achieves strong classification performance, while the early warning mode reaches high recall after only a small number of comments and distinct contributors. The system uses a sliding window to issue warnings when calibrated thresholds are exceeded.

## [World Models Fail in Atari Pong; New Regularization Proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15142) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study evaluates five world models (DreamerV3, DIAMOND, TWISTER, Simulus, STORM) on Atari Pong using a closed-loop rollout diagnostic, revealing systematic failures such as ball disappearance and incorrect motion. The authors propose Concept-Guided Spatial Regularization (CGSReg) to improve world model rollouts and zero-shot MBRL performance. This work highlights critical failure modes in world models that are often masked when evaluated within full MBRL systems, providing a more rigorous diagnostic for model-based RL research. The proposed CGSReg offers a potential path to improve world model fidelity, which could impact applications in robotics, games, and simulation. In pixel-space zero-shot MBRL, DreamerV3's mean return dropped from -5.5 to -20.9 (near the minimum of -21), while other models also underperformed. CGSReg is an auxiliary pixel reconstruction loss applied to segmented concept regions (e.g., the ball in Pong), and it improved rollouts and zero-shot MBRL for DreamerV3, DIAMOND, and TWISTER, but not all models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: World models are neural networks that learn to simulate environment dynamics from experience, used in model-based reinforcement learning (MBRL) to plan or train policies. Atari Pong is a classic benchmark with a simple visual scene, making it easy to spot errors like ball disappearance. The closed-loop rollout diagnostic isolates the world model by using a fixed policy to interact with it, revealing failures that might be compensated by the policy in full MBRL.

## [Plover: Plan-Centric GUI Automation with User Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15193) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Plover introduces a plan-centric vision-based GUI automation system that externalizes task plans as persistent, inspectable, and revisable artifacts, enabling users to supervise and correct agent behavior through localized interventions. This approach addresses a key limitation of current GUI agents where planning remains internal, making it difficult for users to inspect or correct errors. By making plans transparent and editable, Plover enhances controllability and adaptability in real-world GUI automation. Plover uses a planner-executor architecture where the planner generates a step-by-step plan that the executor follows, and users can edit the plan or provide natural-language guidance without restarting the task. A formative study with six participants informed the interaction design, and evaluations show that many autonomous GUI-agent failures are structurally repairable with visible plans.

**Background**: GUI automation agents often fail in dynamic environments due to unexpected dialogs or layout changes. Traditional agents keep planning internal, making it hard for users to understand or fix failures. Plover externalizes plans as editable artifacts, similar to how code editors allow debugging, but for GUI tasks.

## [teLLMe: Causal Analysis of Urban Driving Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15254) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce teLLMe, a system that combines causal structure learning with the PC algorithm, bootstrap stability checks, and LLM-based natural language querying to estimate causal effects from observational urban driving video data. This system enables traffic agencies to answer causal questions like 'How would rain change traffic density?' from observational data, supporting hypothesis generation and expert reasoning without requiring controlled experiments. teLLMe maps natural-language questions to structured causal queries using a schema-aware LLM, and returns a 'Causal Card' summarizing effect estimates, adjustment sets, DAG support, and assumptions. Case studies on BDD-derived traffic events show plausible relationships involving weather, peak hours, and traffic density.

**Background**: Causal inference from observational data is challenging because confounding variables can create spurious correlations. The PC algorithm is a constraint-based method for learning causal DAGs from data, while DoWhy is a Python library that provides a principled four-step interface for causal inference. Bootstrap stability checks help assess the robustness of learned causal structures.

This work addresses a key limitation in current AI dialogue systems that enforce semantic uniformity, offering a path toward more interpretable and cognitively grounded multi-agent systems that balance expressiveness and coherence. MAPS was evaluated on EmpatheticDialogues, TopicalChat, and MultiWOZ datasets, showing it supports semantic alignment without collapsing subjectivity. The framework uses domain-weighted profiles to capture agent-specific cognitive styles and GRU-based dynamic memory for context tracking.

**Background**: Traditional multi-agent dialogue systems often treat all agents as having identical knowledge and reasoning, leading to uniform outputs that lack diversity. ## [T5-CSBoost: Robust LLM Text Detection via Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14113) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose T5-CSBoost, an extension to T5-Sentinel that adds a margin-based triplet loss on decoder embeddings for contrastive style regularization, improving robustness against paraphrasing and edits without changing the T5-small backbone. This work addresses a critical weakness in current LLM text detectors—performance degradation under adversarial perturbations—and achieves state-of-the-art results on multiple benchmarks, making it a practical step toward reliable AI-generated text detection in real-world scenarios. T5-CSBoost retains the next-token prediction objective for source attribution while adding an auxiliary contrastive loss that encourages compact, perturbation-resistant stylistic embeddings. It achieves SOTA on OpenLLMText, HC3, and the MAGE/Deepfake stress-test suite, maintaining robustness under up to 90% adversarial perturbation intensity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: T5-Sentinel is a framework that reframes LLM text detection as a next-token prediction task using the T5 model, avoiding the need for a separate classification head. Contrastive learning, particularly margin-based triplet loss, is a technique that pulls similar samples closer and pushes dissimilar ones apart in embedding space, which helps learn robust representations. This work applies contrastive style regularization to improve the robustness of T5-Sentinel against adversarial perturbations like paraphrasing and character edits.

## [CoEvoT: Co-Evolving CoT for Graph-LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoEvoT introduces a closed-loop framework where graph tokens and textual thoughts co-evolve, enabling step-wise refinement of structural cues for graph-LLM reasoning under distribution shift. This addresses a key limitation of existing CoT-based graph-LLM methods that use fixed graph tokens, potentially improving label-efficient graph learning in dynamic environments. CoEvoT uses a lightweight condition network to update graph token evidence based on intermediate textual thoughts, and feeds updated tokens back into the next reasoning step. Experiments on eight datasets show consistent improvements over state-of-the-art baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Graph learning under distribution shift requires models to adapt to new graphs with limited supervision. Recent graph-LLM approaches linearize graphs into prompts and use LLMs as predictors, often employing Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting for multi-step reasoning. However, existing CoT methods condition on fixed graph tokens, preventing step-wise refinement of structural cues.

## [vGLP: A Language for Human-Machine Distributed Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14138) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces volition-guarded GLP (vGLP), a programming language with operational semantics that extends GLP with volition-guarded clauses, enabling computations driven by human volitions in distributed systems. vGLP formalizes the role of human volition in distributed computations, bridging the gap between human-computer interaction and multi-agent systems, which could lead to more intuitive and consent-driven applications like social networks and digital currencies. The operational semantics of vGLP is defined as an instance of Communicating Volitional Agents, where each reduction step requires the person to will the clause. The paper demonstrates the approach by generating vGLP programs for grassroots social graph, social network, and currencies from volition-guarded multiagent atomic transactions.

**Background**: Volition-Guarded Multiagent Atomic Transactions were previously proposed as an abstract specification language for systems where people and their machines act as agents, and transactions are guarded by both machine states and personal volitions. vGLP extends the existing GLP language with volition-guarded clauses to provide a concrete programming language with operational semantics for such systems.

Researchers propose RENEW, a method that uses human preferences over imagined rollouts to repair world model exploitation in offline reinforcement learning, introducing Dynamics Learning from Human Feedback (DLHF) with an epistemic uncertainty sampling strategy to improve sample efficiency. This work offers a new paradigm for addressing model exploitation in offline model-based RL without requiring expensive expert demonstrations or overly conservative algorithms, potentially enabling safer and more generalizable deployment of world models in real-world applications. RENEW formalizes DLHF using a Bradley-Terry preference loss over trajectory log-likelihoods, and uses epistemic uncertainty to focus fine-tuning on regions where the model is most exploitable, improving sample efficiency and limiting catastrophic forgetting.

## [New Framework Models Saturation and Escape in Knowledge Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14185) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a three-level operational framework using transition kernels and Lyapunov drift to analyze saturation and structural escape in closed-loop knowledge systems. This framework provides a formal, falsifiable way to understand why feedback-driven AI systems (like LLMs and RL agents) plateau and how external interventions can push them beyond current limits, potentially guiding more effective training and discovery algorithms. The framework defines knowledge states evolving via transition kernels indexed by a structural parameter, with attractors and basins as fixed-parameter dynamics. It uses a Lyapunov drift condition to show stable dynamics approach bounded stability regions with exponential transients and a noise-controlled residual floor.

## [Git as Memory Backbone for Agentic Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14390) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper argues that memory for coding agents should be built directly into Git version control, using commits, rebuilds, merges, and reviews as ground truth and verification mechanisms. The proposed system achieves 0.31 pooled MRR in retrieval and 0.83 answer-sufficiency on real developer questions. This approach could eliminate the need for separate memory infrastructure for AI coding agents, reducing complexity and cost while ensuring ground truth and verifiability. It directly addresses the growing challenge of preserving reasoning context in agentic development lifecycles. The system uses a router that dispatches queries to a git-anchored structural map, confidence-gated episodes, or decision synthesis, achieving 382-980 tokens per question. All results are replicable on any user's history at zero labeling cost because ground truth is mined from commit-session links.

## [Rollout-Based Training for Constrained Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new fine-tuning framework called IMCD (Integration Matters for Constrained Diffusion) uses online rollout during training to align constraint guidance with the sampling process, improving constraint satisfaction in diffusion models. This method addresses a key misalignment between training and sampling in constrained diffusion models, potentially enabling more reliable generation of feasible samples in domains like drug discovery and materials design. The approach differentiates through the fixed noise schedule used for numerical integration of the denoising process, exposing the model to violations along the denoising trajectory during training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Constrained generative models aim to produce samples that satisfy complex constraints while staying faithful to the data distribution. Existing methods either optimize constraints during training (which may not match sampling dynamics) or correct samples at inference time (which can cause distribution shift). Diffusion models generate data by gradually denoising random noise through a learned reverse process.

## [ConFlow: Constraint-Guided Flow Matching for Motion Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14424) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ConFlow introduces a constraint-guided flow matching framework that integrates task-specific constraints directly into the training objective using differentiable barrier or cost functions, and replaces the standard Gaussian source distribution with a conditional Gaussian Process to enforce smoothness and boundary conditions. This approach addresses a key limitation of existing flow matching methods that only enforce constraints at inference time, improving constraint satisfaction and trajectory quality for robot motion generation without requiring additional expert data. ConFlow also uses infeasible demonstrations as negative supervision to further improve constraint satisfaction. Experiments on a two-robot navigation task show lower collision rates and higher trajectory quality compared to standard flow matching baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching is a generative modeling paradigm that trains an ODE-based neural sampler by regressing empirical flow fields of data samples. In robot motion generation, additional constraints often exist that are not captured in the training data, and previous methods typically enforce these constraints only at inference time, leading to a training-inference gap.

Researchers propose VTM-Nav, a training-free VLM navigation framework that introduces a hierarchical Visual-Topological Memory (VTM) for cross-episode object-goal navigation, allowing agents to reuse experience across episodes in the same scene. This work addresses a key limitation of episodic protocols in embodied AI by enabling experience reuse, which is more practical for real-world deployment. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on three benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of structured memory for navigation. VTM-Nav organizes scene knowledge at room and object levels, retrieves relevant experience via coarse-to-fine matching, and uses a conservative execution guard to prevent oscillations and premature stopping. It outperforms a strengthened WMNav baseline on HM3D v0.1, HM3D v0.2, and MP3D datasets.

AG-SCL achieves state-of-the-art performance on PTB-XL and a nocturnal ECG dataset, potentially improving clinical diagnosis for underrepresented cardiac conditions. ## [APAL Loss Improves Peak-Critical Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14871) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Asymmetric Peak-Aware Loss (APAL), a model-agnostic loss function that penalizes under-predictions more heavily and increases training weight on peak regions, along with a peak-critical evaluation protocol. This addresses a critical gap in time series forecasting where symmetric losses like MSE fail to capture rare demand spikes, which are crucial for operational tasks such as crowd demand forecasting. APAL offers a simple, plug-and-play solution that can be applied to any forecasting model. APAL combines an asymmetric penalty for under-prediction with adaptive within-horizon thresholding to focus on peak regions. The evaluation protocol includes channel-wise tail error (Top-10%, Top-1%) and peak metrics (precision, recall, F1 under timing tolerance, and peak timing error).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Time series forecasting often uses symmetric loss functions like MSE or MAE, which treat over- and under-predictions equally. In many real-world applications, such as predicting pedestrian demand, under-predicting a peak can lead to severe operational failures. Traditional aggregate metrics like MAE can mask poor performance on rare but critical peaks.

## [LLMs vs. XMLC: Benchmark on German Subject Indexing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14882) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new benchmark study compares supervised extreme multi-label classification (XMLC) methods and LLM-based generative approaches for automated subject indexing of German scientific literature at the German National Library (DNB). This study provides practical insights for libraries considering AI-based subject indexing, highlighting that LLM-based methods perform better on long-tail vocabulary, which is a critical challenge for real-world indexing systems. Supervised XMLC algorithms using transformer-based dense features achieved the best overall binary relevance metrics, but LLM-based generative methods excelled in graded relevance and long-tail vocabulary performance, making them a promising alternative.

**Background**: Extreme multi-label classification (XMLC) deals with labeling data points from an extremely large set of possible labels, often in the thousands or more. Subject indexing in libraries involves assigning controlled vocabulary terms to documents to describe their content. Long-tail vocabulary refers to rare or infrequently used terms that are hard for models to learn due to limited training examples.

## [Random Logit Scaling Defends Against Black-Box Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14921) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Random Logit Scaling (RLS), a lightweight, plug-and-play defense that randomly scales logits to falsify output scores, confusing black-box score-based adversarial attacks while preserving model accuracy. This defense addresses a critical security vulnerability in deep neural networks deployed in real-world scenarios, offering a practical, low-cost solution that can be easily integrated into existing models without significant accuracy loss. RLS is a post-processing defense that randomly scales logits before softmax, significantly reducing attack success rates against state-of-the-art black-box score-based attacks while minimizing confidence score distortion compared to prior randomization defenses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial examples are inputs crafted to fool machine learning models. In black-box score-based attacks, the attacker only has access to the model's output scores (e.g., class probabilities) and iteratively queries the model to generate adversarial examples. Logits are the raw, unnormalized outputs of a neural network's final layer, which are typically converted to probabilities via softmax.

This approach outperforms existing detection baselines consistently across benchmarks, offering a more robust method for identifying AI-generated content in an era of increasingly sophisticated language models. GTCL segments documents into ordered local units, encodes each in an embedding space, and applies contrastive learning to the resulting trajectories to learn geometric regularities of autoregressive generation.

Researchers propose NIFA, a novel FPGA architecture that integrates ADC-free ReRAM-based in-memory computing blocks using analog content-addressable memories (ACAMs) to natively support nonlinear operations and dynamic matrix-matrix multiplication, extending IMC benefits to Transformer models. This work addresses a key limitation of prior FPGA+IMC designs that only accelerate static-weight models, enabling efficient inference for Transformer-based models that dominate modern AI workloads, potentially improving energy and area efficiency by up to 40x and 4.1x respectively. NIFA replaces power-hungry ADCs (which consume >70% area and power in conventional IMC blocks) with ACAMs that perform nonlinear operations in the analog domain, and introduces an FPGA-aware design-space exploration to optimize crossbar dimensions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: ReRAM-based analog in-memory computing performs vector-matrix multiplication directly inside memory crossbars, dramatically reducing data movement and improving energy efficiency. However, conventional IMC only supports static-weight operations, while Transformer models require frequent nonlinear functions and dynamic matrix multiplications, limiting IMC benefits. NIFA overcomes this by integrating ACAMs that can handle both nonlinear and dynamic operations within the IMC block.

## [LEKIA 2.0: Situated LLM Architecture for Emotional Support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25031) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LEKIA 2.0, a situated LLM architecture that separates cognitive and executive layers to maintain temporal continuity and user consent boundaries in multi-turn emotional support dialogues. This addresses a fundamental limitation of standard LLMs—their stateless nature—in process-oriented emotional support, enabling more stable, controllable, and ethically sound AI companions. The architecture could improve the reliability of AI in therapeutic and high-risk human interactions. LEKIA 2.0 decouples situational modeling from intervention execution via a dual-layer design, and introduces a Static-to-Dynamic online evaluation protocol for multi-turn interaction. It achieved an average absolute improvement of approximately 31% over prompt-only baselines in deep intervention loop completion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Standard LLMs operate via next-token prediction over local context, lacking persistent memory of user state and consent boundaries across turns. This statelessness causes premature advancement and boundary violations in multi-turn emotional support. LEKIA 2.0 builds on the earlier LEKIA framework (Layered Expert Knowledge Injection Architecture) by adding a cognitive layer for situational modeling and an executive layer for intervention control.

## [Data-Centric Optimization for Lakehouse Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01185) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a data-centric optimization pipeline for coding agents on a branching lakehouse (Bauplan), transforming agent evaluation from output-matching to state-verification. The pipeline generates task-verifier pairs, executes skills in sandboxes, and scores trajectories using trace signals and programmatic checks, achieving up to 28.6% improvement in held-out reward. This work shifts the focus from model-centric to data-centric optimization for AI agents, which is crucial as agents increasingly interact with data infrastructure. It demonstrates that write-path data workflows provide a rich substrate for optimizing agent skills, potentially improving reliability and performance in real-world data systems. The pipeline is evaluated on hundreds of tasks using Bauplan, a branching lakehouse with Git-like primitives. Optimized skills show up to 28.6% improvement in held-out reward, suggesting that state-verification is a more effective evaluation paradigm than output-matching for coding agents.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: A branching lakehouse is a data architecture that treats data and metadata as versioned, mergeable assets using Git-like commits, branches, and merges. Bauplan is a serverless data platform that implements this concept, providing headless APIs and isolated branches for safe agent operations. Data-centric optimization focuses on improving the data and artifacts (like skill files) that teach agents, rather than only tuning model parameters.

This paper introduces reliability-conditioned belief updating for LLM agents, which estimates per-observation reliability from epistemic language and outperforms naive methods on contradictory evidence. It also proposes a provenance-capped poisoning defense that resists volumetric memory poisoning attacks. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent memory design by showing when probabilistic belief-based memory is beneficial, specifically in environments with conflicting and differently trustworthy evidence. The findings have practical implications for building more robust and trustworthy LLM agents. The paper introduces Nous, a long-term memory architecture using closed-form Bayesian inference with information-theoretic surprise and entropy-based forgetting. A 27.5-point discrepancy between token-F1 and LLM-as-judge evaluation highlights reproducibility concerns for long-term memory benchmarks.

This paper investigates when such probabilistic memory is actually helpful.

Researchers introduced FEP-Nav, a biologically inspired framework that applies the Free Energy Principle to enable real-time perceptual adaptation in deep neural networks for robust visual navigation under sensory corruption. This work addresses a key limitation of deep neural networks—their lack of real-time plasticity—by providing a principled way to adapt to abrupt sensory shifts without gradient updates, potentially improving the robustness of autonomous navigation systems in real-world environments. FEP-Nav combines a Top-down Decoder that generates internal expectations of uncorrupted sensory input with Adaptive Normalization that shifts corrupted feature distributions toward prior statistics, approximating variational free energy minimization during inference.

## [SARA: Robust Preference-Based RL via Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12529) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Similarity as Reward Alignment (SARA), a contrastive framework for preference-based reinforcement learning that learns latent representations of preferred samples and computes rewards as similarities to that latent, achieving robustness to noisy labels and adaptability to diverse feedback formats. SARA addresses a critical limitation in preference-based RL—labeler errors—by providing robustness to noisy labels, which is essential for real-world applications where non-experts provide feedback. Its versatility across feedback formats also broadens the applicability of PbRL in aligning AI systems with human intent. On continuous control offline RL benchmarks with varying realistic noise rates, SARA showed competitive and more stable performance with statistically significant improvements over baselines (Wilcoxon signed-rank, p < 0.01). The computed rewards also displayed higher correlation with environment rewards across noise rates compared to baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Preference-based Reinforcement Learning (PbRL) avoids manual reward engineering by using human preferences as the reward signal. However, existing PbRL methods often assume high-quality feedback from experts, making them brittle to noisy labels from non-expert labelers. Contrastive learning, which learns representations by pulling similar samples together and pushing dissimilar ones apart, provides a natural way to extract robust features from preference data.

## [PRISM: Phase-Based Neural Network Suppresses Noise via Interference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.01208) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced PRISM, a complex-valued neural architecture that uses phase encoding and subtractive interference in the frequency domain to suppress noise, achieving better parameter efficiency and representation quality than standard Transformers. This work demonstrates that phase can serve as a computationally useful mechanism for disentangling semantic importance from activation magnitude, potentially leading to more efficient and interpretable neural sequence models. PRISM enforces a strict unit-norm constraint on complex-valued activations and replaces attention with gated harmonic convolutions, forcing the model to rely on phase-based interference rather than magnitude gating. Interventional ablations show that preserving phase maintains performance, while disrupting phase causes severe degradation.

Subtractive interference in the frequency domain is a technique used in signal processing to cancel noise by exploiting phase relationships.

## [REST: Tree-Structured Paths for Zero-Shot Object-Goal Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18624) ⭐️ 7.0/10

REST introduces a receding horizon explorative Steiner tree approach that uses tree-structured paths instead of single waypoints as subgoal candidates for zero-shot object-goal navigation. It builds an open-vocabulary 3D map, grows an agent-centric tree of safe paths, and selects the next-best path via chain-of-thought LLM reasoning. This work addresses a key limitation of prior zero-shot navigation methods that treat subgoal candidates as isolated waypoints, missing en-route information gain. By modeling the option space as a tree of paths, REST enables coarse-to-fine reasoning and achieves top performance on multiple benchmarks without task-specific training. REST is a training-free framework that uses a receding horizon planning strategy to grow a Steiner tree of paths from the agent's current position. It textualizes each branch into a spatial narrative for LLM reasoning, and consistently ranks among top methods on Gibson, HM3D, and HSSD benchmarks in success rate and path efficiency.

**Background**: Zero-shot object-goal navigation (ZSON) requires an agent to find a target object in an unknown environment without any task-specific training. Prior hierarchical approaches decompose the problem into scene understanding (belief) and decision-making (policy), with subgoal candidates typically represented as single waypoints scored by destination utility. The Steiner tree problem is a classic combinatorial optimization problem that seeks the minimum-weight tree connecting a set of terminals, and its variants are used here to generate tree-structured path options.

## [VAN-AD: Visual MAE with Normalizing Flow for Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26842) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes VAN-AD, a framework that adapts a visual Masked Autoencoder (MAE) pretrained on ImageNet to time series anomaly detection, introducing an Adaptive Distribution Mapping Module and a Normalizing Flow Module to address overgeneralization and limited local perception. This work demonstrates the feasibility of cross-modal transfer from vision to time series for anomaly detection, potentially reducing the need for dataset-specific training and improving generalization across diverse IoT scenarios. The Adaptive Distribution Mapping Module maps reconstruction results before and after MAE into a unified statistical space to amplify anomaly discrepancies, while the Normalizing Flow Module estimates probability density of the current window under the global distribution. Experiments on nine real-world datasets show consistent outperformance over state-of-the-art methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Time series anomaly detection is crucial for IoT system reliability, but traditional methods require per-dataset training and generalize poorly. Foundation models like LLMs have been explored, but face cross-modal gaps. Visual MAE pretrained on ImageNet captures general patterns that may transfer to time series, as shown in prior work like VisionTS for forecasting.

## [N-ary Crossbar IMC with MTJs Achieves 93.56% MNIST Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26979) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present a simulation framework for N-ary crossbar in-memory computing using magnetic tunnel junctions, achieving 93.56% accuracy on MNIST classification with a 4x4 array of 4-state MTJs. This work demonstrates the feasibility of using multibit MTJ crossbars for energy-efficient neural network inference, addressing key error sources like weight quantization and non-idealities, which is crucial for advancing beyond von Neumann architectures. The study identifies weight quantization as the primary error source and finds that cell-specific random noise is less detrimental than systematic errors due to averaging across the array. PCA dimensionality reduction significantly reduces operations with only modest accuracy loss.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: In-memory computing performs analog matrix-vector multiplications directly in memory arrays, bypassing the data movement bottleneck of traditional von Neumann architectures. Magnetic tunnel junctions (MTJs) are emerging devices that can store multiple resistance states, enabling multibit synaptic weights in crossbar arrays. Weight quantization reduces precision to save energy but introduces accuracy trade-offs.

This work addresses a key limitation in semantic watermarking—vulnerability to paragraph-level paraphrasing—and achieves over 30% improvement in detection accuracy over prior methods, making AI-generated text more reliably traceable. SAMark achieves up to 90.2% true positive rate at 1% false positive rate under paragraph-level paraphrasing attacks, while maintaining generation quality comparable to unwatermarked text and breaking the robustness-quality trade-off.

## [Conan-embedding-v3: Fusing Modality-Specific Models for Omni-Modal Embedding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09331) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Conan-embedding-v3 introduces a decouple-fuse-recover framework for omni-modal retrieval, which first trains modality-specific specialists independently, fuses their task vectors into a single backbone, and then applies Projector Recovery to fix a failure mode called Projector Drift for projector-based modalities like audio. This work addresses a critical challenge in building unified multimodal retrieval systems by identifying and solving Projector Drift, a previously unrecognized failure mode when fusing projector-based modalities. It achieves state-of-the-art results on MMEB (74.9) and MAEB audio suite (55.61), advancing the field of omni-modal embedding. The decouple-fuse-recover framework consists of Decoupled Specialist Fusion (training specialists independently and fusing task vectors) and Projector Recovery (full-parameter fine-tuning of the projector with frozen backbone) followed by balanced multi-modal rehearsal. The model supports retrieval across text, image, video, document, and audio modalities in a single backbone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Omni-modal retrieval aims to create a single embedding space for multiple modalities like text, image, video, and audio. Traditional approaches often train separate models for each modality or use a shared encoder with modality-specific projectors, but fusing them into one backbone can cause performance degradation due to interference. Task vectors represent the difference between a fine-tuned model and its pre-trained base, and merging them is a common technique for multi-task model fusion.

This work addresses a critical but overlooked robustness issue in in-context segmentation, where different references can lead to inconsistent results. By reducing variance and achieving state-of-the-art accuracy, CG-ICS could make ICS systems more reliable for real-world applications like medical imaging or autonomous driving. CG-ICS introduces a concept reasoning module that uses an MLLM to propose candidate concepts and a SAM3-driven scoring function with tree-search refinement to select reliable textual concepts. It also includes a parallel visual exemplar route for query-side spatial grounding, and both routes activate a frozen SAM3 backbone.

## [ReOPD: Efficient Multi-Turn On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04763) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose ReOPD (Replayed-Prefix On-Policy Distillation), an off-environment method that reuses teacher trajectories as replayed prefixes to avoid costly environment interactions in multi-turn on-policy distillation for LLM agents. ReOPD reduces training cost by at least 4x per rollout while preserving accuracy, making scalable distillation for agentic tasks more practical and accessible. ReOPD addresses the 'prefix trap' distribution shift by using a step-decaying sampling schedule that emphasizes early, lower-shift prefixes. It achieves zero tool calls during student training and is at least 4x faster per rollout than standard on-policy distillation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) for LLM agents involves a student model imitating a teacher model over multi-turn interactions, but requires costly fresh rollouts and teacher queries. ReOPD reuses pre-collected teacher trajectories as prefixes, allowing the student to act at selected steps while the teacher provides supervision without new environment interactions.

Researchers introduced the RG-Flow Transformer, a novel architecture that incorporates a renormalization-group inductive bias via a scale-aware stream with learnable anomalous dimension and block-spin coarse-graining, and benchmarked it against a vanilla transformer on scarce EEG sleep staging data. While RG-Flow did not outperform vanilla transformers in accuracy, it uniquely recovered the continuous spectral exponent β out-of-sample (R²=0.416), offering interpretability that vanilla models lack, which could advance neuroscience and clinical EEG analysis. On 5-class AASM sleep staging with leave-one-subject-out cross-validation, RG-Flow achieved 77.3% accuracy vs. 77.0% for vanilla (p=0.294), and the predicted scarce-data crossover did not appear—vanilla was numerically ahead at every data budget.

<a id="item-292"></a>
## [IMEX: New Method for Explainable AI with Higher-Order Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14096) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced IMEX (Interaction-Based Model Explanation), a novel framework for explainable predictive modeling that identifies important variables and higher-order interactions without cardinality restrictions. The method is built on two metrics: Static Correlation Power (PCS) for individual features and Interaction Correlation Power (PCI) for non-additive effects. IMEX addresses a key limitation in explainable AI by enabling the detection of higher-order interactions beyond pairwise, which is crucial for understanding complex models in fields like healthcare and finance. This could improve trust and validation of black-box models in critical applications. The PCS component was experimentally validated against INVASE on three synthetic datasets, showing IMEX can recover relevant feature structures under non-linear, conditional, and multicollinear relationships. The method does not impose limitations on the cardinality of feature subsets, allowing analysis of interactions among more than two variables.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Predictive models often act as black boxes, making it hard to understand why they make certain predictions. Traditional explainability methods like SHAP or LIME typically focus on individual feature importance or pairwise interactions, but real-world systems often involve higher-order interactions among multiple features. IMEX aims to fill this gap by providing a framework that can capture these complex relationships without cardinality restrictions.

## [3D Fast-Fading Model for Vehicular Magnetic Induction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04737) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a 3D model for fast-fading in vehicular magnetic induction (VMI) communication, deriving statistical distributions such as CDF, PDF, and expectation of the channel, and introduces a power control algorithm using non-cooperative game and multiagent Q-learning. This work addresses a previously unmodeled phenomenon—fast-fading in VMI due to antenna vibration—which is critical for reliable vehicular communication in underground environments, and provides a foundation for optimizing network throughput in MI cellular networks. The fast-fading in VMI cannot be modeled by the central limit theorem, unlike traditional wireless fast-fading, and the paper finds that VMI fast-fading leads to more uniformly distributed channel coefficients. The proposed power control algorithm uses non-cooperative game and multiagent Q-learning to optimize throughput.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Magnetic induction (MI) communication uses near-field magnetic fields for short-range wireless links, often in underground environments. Traditional MI channels are considered quasi-static with no fast-fading, but in vehicular MI (VMI), antenna vibration introduces fast-fading that differs from conventional wireless fading. This paper provides the first 3D model for this phenomenon.

## [Planar Proof Advances Thomas Positive Circuits Conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14143) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper uses planar dynamical systems tools to prove conditions under which Thomas' conjecture—that positive circuits are necessary for multistationarity—holds for planar systems. The result strengthens the theoretical foundation linking circuit structure to multistationarity, which is crucial for understanding biological systems like cell differentiation and signaling networks. The proof relies on planar analysis and dynamical system techniques; it does not claim the conjecture holds for all systems, only for planar ones under specific conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Thomas' conjecture, proposed by René Thomas, states that a positive circuit (a cyclic influence with an even number of negative edges) is necessary for a dynamical system to exhibit multistationarity (multiple stable steady states). Planar dynamical systems are those defined on a two-dimensional phase space. This work addresses the conjecture for such systems.

## [ViPSAM: Visual Prompting for Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14328) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ViPSAM adapts the Segment Anything Model (SAM) for medical image segmentation by using contrast-enhanced MRI as a visual prompt to improve lesion delineation on non-contrast CT, outperforming existing U-Net and SAM-based methods on liver lesion segmentation for proton therapy planning. This work demonstrates a practical cross-modality prompting approach that leverages readily available contrast-enhanced MRI to boost segmentation accuracy on non-contrast CT, potentially improving proton therapy planning and reducing the need for contrast agents in CT scans. ViPSAM introduces a visual prompt encoder to extract guidance features from contrast-enhanced images and a visual-guided cross-attention module to integrate non-contrast and contrast-enhanced features, adapting the SAM mask decoder in a parameter-efficient manner.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: The Segment Anything Model (SAM) is a foundational model for image segmentation that can perform zero-shot segmentation with prompts. In proton therapy planning, non-contrast CT is used for dose calculation but offers poor lesion contrast, while contrast-enhanced MRI provides better delineation. ViPSAM bridges this gap by using MRI as a visual prompt for SAM.

## [Prompt Tuning with Adaptive Focal Loss for MCI Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15047) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a parameter-efficient prompt tuning method using a frozen DINOv2 model with three learnable prompt tokens and an adaptive focal loss that integrates continuous MoCA scores, achieving an MCI-class F1 of 0.641 and AUC of 0.795 on drawing test data. This work addresses data scarcity and class imbalance in automated MCI screening, offering an interpretable and parameter-efficient alternative to computationally expensive fully fine-tuned models, which could improve early detection of Alzheimer's disease. The method uses only 1.19 million trainable parameters, with each prompt token serving as a query in a shared cross-attention layer over image patch tokens, and spatial explainability is achieved directly through attention maps. The adaptive focal loss generalizes standard soft-label approaches by incorporating continuous cognitive scores.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 17, 04:00

**Background**: Mild Cognitive Impairment (MCI) is an early stage of cognitive decline that often precedes Alzheimer's disease. Automated screening from drawing tests is challenging due to limited data and class imbalance. Prompt tuning is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adapts large pretrained models by learning a small set of trainable vectors (soft prompts) while keeping the base model frozen. DINOv2 is a self-supervised vision foundation model by Meta AI that produces high-performance visual features without fine-tuning.

Researchers propose an adaptive encoder freezing strategy for federated learning in MRI-to-CT conversion, reducing training time, energy consumption, and CO2 emissions by up to 23% while maintaining model performance. This work addresses the resource disparity in federated learning, enabling institutions with limited computational infrastructure to participate in collaborative medical imaging AI without sacrificing performance, advancing Green AI and healthcare equity.
